gpt-6.1-sol/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

gpt-6.1-sol/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from heapq import heappop, heappush
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Order jobs by dependencies, then ready-job priority and input position.

    Repeated dependencies count once. Invalid IDs, missing dependencies, and
    dependency cycles raise ScheduleError without modifying the supplied jobs.
    """
    jobs = list(jobs)
    positions = {}
    for index, job in enumerate(jobs):
        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Job at input position {index} has invalid ID {job.id!r}; "
                "IDs must be non-empty strings"
            )
        if job.id in positions:
            raise ScheduleError(f"Duplicate job ID: {job.id!r}")
        positions[job.id] = index

    remaining = [0] * len(jobs)
    dependents = [[] for _ in jobs]
    for index, job in enumerate(jobs):
        seen = set()
        for dependency in job.depends_on:
            if not isinstance(dependency, str) or not dependency:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} has invalid dependency {dependency!r}; "
                    "dependencies must name supplied jobs with non-empty string IDs"
                )
            if dependency not in positions:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} has unknown dependency {dependency!r}"
                )
            if dependency not in seen:
                seen.add(dependency)
                remaining[index] += 1
                dependents[positions[dependency]].append(index)

    ready = []
    for index, job in enumerate(jobs):
        if remaining[index] == 0:
            heappush(ready, (-job.priority, index))

    order = []
    while ready:
        _, index = heappop(ready)
        order.append(jobs[index].id)
        for dependent in dependents[index]:
            remaining[dependent] -= 1
            if remaining[dependent] == 0:
                heappush(ready, (-jobs[dependent].priority, dependent))

    if len(order) != len(jobs):
        unresolved = ", ".join(
            repr(job.id) for index, job in enumerate(jobs) if remaining[index]
        )
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"Dependency cycle detected; unresolved jobs: {unresolved}"
        )
    return order

gpt-6.1-sol/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

from copy import deepcopy
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])
        self.assertIn("build", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("missing", str(raised.exception))

    def test_empty_input(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(iter(())), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])

    def test_invalid_ids_are_rejected(self):
        for invalid_id in ("", None, 0, False, [], {}, b"job"):
            with self.subTest(id=invalid_id):
                with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
                    plan_jobs([Job(invalid_id)])
                self.assertIn("non-empty strings", str(raised.exception))
                self.assertIn("position 0", str(raised.exception))

    def test_duplicate_ids_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs([Job("same", 1), Job("same", 2)])
        self.assertIn("Duplicate", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("same", str(raised.exception))

    def test_invalid_dependency_ids_are_rejected(self):
        for dependency in ("", None, 0, False, [], {}, b"source"):
            with self.subTest(dependency=dependency):
                with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
                    plan_jobs([
                        Job("source"),
                        Job("consumer", depends_on=(dependency,)),
                    ])
                self.assertIn("consumer", str(raised.exception))
                self.assertIn("invalid dependency", str(raised.exception))

    def test_valid_ids_need_not_be_identifiers(self):
        jobs = [Job("完成", depends_on=(" ",)), Job(" ")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [" ", "完成"])

    def test_dependencies_can_refer_to_later_input(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("last", depends_on=("middle",)),
            Job("middle", depends_on=("first",)),
            Job("first"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["first", "middle", "last"])

    def test_newly_ready_job_can_preempt_existing_ready_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("waiting", 2),
            Job("unlock", 3),
            Job("urgent", 100, depends_on=("unlock",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["unlock", "urgent", "waiting"])

    def test_newly_ready_tie_uses_original_input_order(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("earlier", 5, depends_on=("unlock",)),
            Job("waiting", 5),
            Job("unlock", 10),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["unlock", "earlier", "waiting"])

    def test_ties_do_not_use_lexical_id_order(self):
        self.assertEqual(
            plan_jobs([Job("z"), Job("a"), Job("m")]), ["z", "a", "m"]
        )

    def test_negative_priorities(self):
        jobs = [Job("low", -10), Job("high", -1), Job("tied", -1)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["high", "tied", "low"])

    def test_fan_in_fan_out_and_disconnected_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("finish", 100, depends_on=("left", "right")),
            Job("right", 2, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("independent", 0),
            Job("left", 1, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("root", -1),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(result, ["independent", "root", "right", "left", "finish"])
        self.assertCountEqual(result, [job.id for job in jobs])
        positions = {job_id: index for index, job_id in enumerate(result)}
        for job in jobs:
            for dependency in job.depends_on:
                self.assertLess(positions[dependency], positions[job.id])

    def test_repeated_dependencies_count_once(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("finish", depends_on=("source", "other", "source", "other")),
            Job("source"),
            Job("other"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["source", "other", "finish"])

    def test_generator_input(self):
        jobs = (job for job in [Job("b", depends_on=("a",)), Job("a")])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs([Job("self", depends_on=("self",))])
        self.assertIn("cycle", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("self", str(raised.exception))

    def test_two_job_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs([
                Job("alpha", depends_on=("beta",)),
                Job("beta", depends_on=("alpha",)),
            ])
        self.assertIn("cycle", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("alpha", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("beta", str(raised.exception))

    def test_cycle_with_independent_and_downstream_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("alpha", depends_on=("gamma",)),
            Job("beta", depends_on=("alpha",)),
            Job("gamma", depends_on=("beta",)),
            Job("blocked", depends_on=("gamma",)),
            Job("independent", 100),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(raised.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle", message)
        for job_id in ("alpha", "beta", "gamma", "blocked"):
            self.assertIn(job_id, message)
        self.assertNotIn("independent", message)

    def test_missing_dependency_is_validated_even_in_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as raised:
            plan_jobs([Job("loop", depends_on=("loop", "missing"))])
        self.assertIn("unknown dependency", str(raised.exception))
        self.assertIn("missing", str(raised.exception))

    def test_success_does_not_mutate_input(self):
        dependencies = ["source", "source"]
        payload = {"nested": [1, 2, 3]}
        jobs = [
            Job("consumer", 100, depends_on=dependencies, payload=payload),
            Job("source"),
        ]
        before = deepcopy(jobs)
        original_jobs = tuple(jobs)
        for _ in range(2):
            self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["source", "consumer"])
            self.assertEqual(jobs, before)
        self.assertTrue(all(job is original for job, original in zip(jobs, original_jobs)))
        self.assertIs(jobs[0].depends_on, dependencies)
        self.assertIs(jobs[0].payload, payload)

    def test_failure_does_not_mutate_input(self):
        for dependencies in (["missing"], ["consumer"]):
            with self.subTest(dependencies=dependencies):
                jobs = [Job("consumer", depends_on=dependencies), Job("source")]
                before = deepcopy(jobs)
                with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
                    plan_jobs(jobs)
                self.assertEqual(jobs, before)
                self.assertIs(jobs[0].depends_on, dependencies)

    def test_long_dependency_chain(self):
        jobs = [
            Job(f"job-{index}", depends_on=(f"job-{index - 1}",))
            for index in range(1499, 0, -1)
        ]
        jobs.append(Job("job-0"))
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [f"job-{index}" for index in range(1500)])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

gpt-6-astra/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Dependency-aware scheduler

All deliverables are complete: implemented plan_jobs in jobqueue.py, expanded test_jobqueue.py, ran the tests, and recorded the design and results here. Only Python standard-library modules are used. All repository reads and changes were confined to this task directory, and no network access was used.

Design

The scheduler materializes the input iterable once, validates non-empty string IDs and uniqueness, then validates dependencies against the complete ID index. Errors identify the invalid value and, where applicable, its owning job. IDs are used exactly as supplied; whitespace is not stripped. Duplicate dependencies represent a single prerequisite.

Kahn's topological sort tracks each job's outstanding prerequisites and reverse dependency edges. A heap keyed by negative priority and original input index selects the next ready job. Newly unblocked jobs enter the heap immediately, so every choice respects priority and input-order ties across all ready jobs. Scheduling is iterative and handles long chains without recursion.

If jobs remain blocked after the heap empties, ScheduleError reports a cycle and lists blocked IDs in input order. This list includes cycle members and may also include their downstream dependents; it is labeled as blocked jobs rather than an exact cycle path. All scheduling state is kept in separate collections; supplied jobs, dependency lists, and payloads are not modified.

For V jobs and E supplied dependency entries, time is O(V + E + V log V) and auxiliary space is O(V + E).

Edge cases covered

  • Empty and single-job input, generator input, and a reversed 1,500-job chain.
  • Empty, non-string, unhashable, and duplicate IDs; unchanged whitespace and Unicode IDs.
  • Unknown, empty, non-string, and unhashable dependencies, including invalid jobs in otherwise schedulable input.
  • Negative priorities, ties resolved by input order rather than ID or ready time, and newly ready jobs competing immediately with waiting jobs.
  • Multiple prerequisites, shared prerequisites, and repeated dependency names.
  • Self-cycles, multi-job cycles, downstream blocked jobs, and a disconnected ready component alongside a cycle.
  • Non-mutation on success and failure, preservation of object identity and payloads, and repeatable results across repeated calls.
  • An independent exhaustive oracle for 64 four-job DAGs: enumerate every valid permutation and compare the scheduler with the best priority/input-order ranking. This also checks completeness and dependency ordering.

Verification

Working directory: /Users/arda/projects/agent-eval-runs/agent-eval-gpt-6-astra/01-feature-building

Exact command:

python3 -B -m unittest -v test_jobqueue

Result: exit code 0; all 25 tests passed, including the oracle's 64 graph cases. The command's final output was:

gpt-6-astra/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

gpt-6-astra/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from heapq import heapify, heappop, heappush
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Order jobs by dependencies, then ready priority and original input order.

    Repeated dependencies count as one prerequisite. Cycle diagnostics list
    blocked jobs, which can include downstream dependents of the cycle.
    """
    jobs = list(jobs)
    indices: dict[str, int] = {}
    for index, job in enumerate(jobs):
        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Job at input index {index} must have a non-empty string ID; "
                f"got {job.id!r}"
            )
        if job.id in indices:
            raise ScheduleError(f"Duplicate job ID: {job.id!r}")
        indices[job.id] = index

    remaining = [0] * len(jobs)
    dependents: list[list[int]] = [[] for _ in jobs]
    for index, job in enumerate(jobs):
        seen: set[str] = set()
        for dependency in job.depends_on:
            if not isinstance(dependency, str) or not dependency:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} has invalid dependency {dependency!r}; "
                    "expected a non-empty string job ID"
                )
            if dependency not in indices:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} has unknown dependency {dependency!r}"
                )
            if dependency not in seen:
                seen.add(dependency)
                remaining[index] += 1
                dependents[indices[dependency]].append(index)

    ready = [
        (-job.priority, index)
        for index, job in enumerate(jobs)
        if remaining[index] == 0
    ]
    heapify(ready)
    ordered: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        _, index = heappop(ready)
        ordered.append(jobs[index].id)
        for dependent in dependents[index]:
            remaining[dependent] -= 1
            if remaining[dependent] == 0:
                heappush(ready, (-jobs[dependent].priority, dependent))

    if len(ordered) != len(jobs):
        blocked = ", ".join(
            repr(jobs[index].id)
            for index, count in enumerate(remaining)
            if count > 0
        )
        raise ScheduleError(f"Dependency cycle detected; blocked job IDs: {blocked}")
    return ordered

gpt-6-astra/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

from copy import deepcopy
from itertools import combinations, permutations
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_empty_input(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(iter(())), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])

    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "build.*missing"):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_invalid_job_ids_are_rejected(self):
        for invalid_id in ("", None, 42, True, [], {}):
            with self.subTest(job_id=invalid_id):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "non-empty string ID"):
                    plan_jobs([Job(invalid_id)])

    def test_duplicate_job_ids_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "Duplicate.*same"):
            plan_jobs([Job("same", 1), Job("same", 100)])

    def test_invalid_dependencies_are_rejected(self):
        for dependency in ("", None, 42, True, [], {}):
            with self.subTest(dependency=dependency):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "build.*invalid dependency"):
                    plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=(dependency,))])

    def test_all_components_are_validated(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "broken.*missing"):
            plan_jobs([Job("ready", 100), Job("broken", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_nonempty_ids_are_not_normalized(self):
        jobs = [Job(" a "), Job("a"), Job(" "), Job("猫")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [" a ", "a", " ", "猫"])

    def test_equal_priorities_use_input_order_not_id_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("z"), Job("m"), Job("a")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["z", "m", "a"])

    def test_negative_priorities(self):
        jobs = [Job("low", -10), Job("medium", -1), Job("high", 0)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["high", "medium", "low"])

    def test_newly_ready_job_preempts_lower_priority_job(self):
        jobs = [Job("waiting", 1), Job("root", 2), Job("child", 100, ("root",))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "child", "waiting"])

    def test_newly_ready_tie_uses_original_order_not_ready_time(self):
        jobs = [Job("child", 1, ("root",)), Job("waiting", 1), Job("root", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "child", "waiting"])

    def test_simultaneously_ready_jobs_use_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("z", 1, ("root",)), Job("a", 1, ("root",)), Job("root")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "z", "a"])

    def test_multiple_dependencies_and_shared_prerequisite(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("finish", 100, ("left", "right")),
            Job("left", 2, ("root",)),
            Job("right", 1, ("root",)),
            Job("root"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "left", "right", "finish"])

    def test_repeated_dependencies_count_once(self):
        jobs = [Job("child", 100, ("root", "root", "other")), Job("root"), Job("other")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "other", "child"])

    def test_generator_input(self):
        jobs = (job for job in [Job("child", 5, ("root",)), Job("root")])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "child"])

    def test_long_reverse_ordered_chain(self):
        jobs = [
            Job(str(index), depends_on=(str(index - 1),) if index else ())
            for index in reversed(range(1500))
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [str(index) for index in range(1500)])

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "cycle.*self"):
            plan_jobs([Job("self", depends_on=("self",))])

    def test_cycle_diagnostic_includes_involved_ids(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("alpha", depends_on=("gamma",)),
            Job("beta", depends_on=("alpha",)),
            Job("gamma", depends_on=("beta",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as caught:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(caught.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle", message)
        for job in jobs:
            self.assertIn(job.id, message)

    def test_cycle_with_downstream_and_disconnected_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("ready", 100),
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("downstream", depends_on=("b",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as caught:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(caught.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle", message)
        self.assertIn("'a'", message)
        self.assertIn("'b'", message)
        self.assertIn("blocked job IDs", message)
        self.assertNotIn("'ready'", message)

    def test_jobs_dependencies_and_payloads_are_not_mutated(self):
        dependencies = ["root", "root"]
        payload = {"items": [1, 2]}
        jobs = [Job("child", 10, dependencies, payload), Job("root")]
        snapshot = deepcopy(jobs)
        original_jobs = tuple(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "child"])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "child"])
        self.assertEqual(jobs, snapshot)
        for original, current in zip(original_jobs, jobs):
            self.assertIs(original, current)
        self.assertIs(jobs[0].depends_on, dependencies)
        self.assertIs(jobs[0].payload, payload)

    def test_invalid_input_is_not_mutated(self):
        cases = [
            [Job("a", depends_on=["b"]), Job("b", depends_on=["a"])],
            [Job("a", depends_on=["missing"])],
            [Job("a"), Job("a")],
        ]
        for jobs in cases:
            with self.subTest(jobs=jobs):
                snapshot = deepcopy(jobs)
                with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
                    plan_jobs(jobs)
                self.assertEqual(jobs, snapshot)

    def test_small_dags_match_best_valid_permutation(self):
        # An independent oracle: enumerate valid schedules and pick the best
        # lexicographic priority/input-order ranking, without simulating a queue.
        ids = ("a", "b", "c", "d")
        possible_edges = list(combinations(ids, 2))
        input_order = ("d", "b", "a", "c")
        priorities = {"a": 2, "b": -1, "c": 2, "d": 5}
        for mask in range(1 << len(possible_edges)):
            with self.subTest(edge_mask=mask):
                edges = [
                    edge for bit, edge in enumerate(possible_edges)
                    if mask & (1 << bit)
                ]
                jobs = [
                    Job(job_id, priorities[job_id], tuple(
                        source for source, target in edges if target == job_id
                    ))
                    for job_id in input_order
                ]
                valid_orders = [
                    order for order in permutations(ids)
                    if all(order.index(source) < order.index(target) for source, target in edges)
                ]
                expected = min(valid_orders, key=lambda order: [
                    (-priorities[job_id], input_order.index(job_id)) for job_id in order
                ])
                self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), list(expected))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

haiku-5.5/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Response: dependency-aware scheduler (plan_jobs)

Design

plan_jobs in jobqueue.py is Kahn's topological sort driven by a min-heap.

  1. Validate in two passes. The first pass checks each item's type, id, uniqueness, and priority, and records every job's input position. The second pass validates depends_on against the complete set of IDs, so forward references work. Any failure raises ScheduleError before anything is scheduled.
  2. Build the graph. deps maps each job to its unique dependency names in declared order. dependents is the reverse edge list. waiting counts unfinished dependencies per job.
  3. Schedule. Jobs with waiting == 0 go onto a heap keyed by (-priority, input_position, id). Each step pops the best ready job, appends it to the plan, and decrements its dependents. A dependent enters the heap when its count reaches zero. The key matches the spec exactly: higher priority first, ties broken by input order. Because the position is unique, the ID is never compared.
  4. Detect cycles. If the plan is shorter than the input, the leftover jobs are blocked. _find_cycle starts at the first blocked job and follows unfinished dependencies until a job repeats. It reports that loop, not the whole downstream blocked set. The error reads dependency cycle: a -> b -> a; unschedulable jobs: .... Arrows point from a job to one of its dependencies.

Complexity is O((V + E) log V). The function never writes to a Job or to a caller's depends_on container. It only builds new dicts and lists, and depends_on is read with tuple(...).

Choices beyond the spec

  • A whitespace-only id is rejected as well as "", since it is not a useful identifier.
  • priority must be an int. bool is rejected even though it subclasses int, because True as a priority is almost always a mistake. Floats and strings are rejected so that ordering stays total.
  • A bare str for depends_on is rejected with a dedicated message. Otherwise "fetch" would be iterated as "f", "e", ... and produce a confusing "unknown job 'f'" error.
  • Non-Job items are rejected. Lists are accepted for depends_on (the dataclass does not enforce the tuple annotation). Duplicate dependency names are accepted and counted once.
  • A self-dependency (a depends on a) is reported as a cycle, dependency cycle: a -> a.

Edge cases covered (test_jobqueue.py, 26 tests)

The three original tests are unchanged.

Ordering:

  • Empty input returns [].
  • A generator is accepted and consumed once.
  • A job released mid-run with higher priority runs before a lower-priority job that was already ready.
  • Equal-priority released jobs keep input order, not alphabetical order.
  • A diamond dependency runs the shared parent first and the join last.
  • Duplicate dependency names are harmless.
  • A list-valued depends_on works.
  • Randomized check: 25 seeded random DAGs, each compared against a brute-force reference that re-derives the choice at every step. The test also checks that every ID appears once and that every dependency precedes its dependent.

Validation:

  • Duplicate IDs are rejected, with the positions named.
  • Empty, blank, None, int, and list IDs are rejected.
  • An unknown dependency is rejected, and the message names both jobs.
  • Non-string dependencies are rejected.
  • A bare-string depends_on is rejected.
  • A non-iterable depends_on is rejected.
  • Non-Job items are rejected.
  • Non-int priorities (1.5, "high", True) are rejected.

Cycles:

  • Self-dependency.
  • A two-job cycle, with the IDs in the message.
  • A three-job cycle alongside unrelated acyclic jobs. The message names only the loop.
  • A cycle entered from a downstream job still reports the real loop.
  • Jobs blocked by a cycle are listed as unschedulable.

Immutability:

  • Job objects, their order, and the caller's depends_on list are unchanged after a successful call.

Sample messages, from running the code:

dependency cycle: a -> b -> a; unschedulable jobs: c, a, b
job 'build' depends on unknown job 'missing'
duplicate job id 'a' at positions 0 and 1
job 'build' depends_on must be a collection of job IDs, not a string

Verification

Command (run from the task directory; PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 keeps __pycache__ out of it):

Environment: Python 3.14.7, standard library only.

Result: Ran 26 tests in 0.052s followed by OK, exit status 0.

haiku-5.5/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

haiku-5.5/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from heapq import heapify, heappop, heappush
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def _check_job(job: object, position: int, seen: dict[str, int]) -> None:
    if not isinstance(job, Job):
        raise ScheduleError(f"item {position} is {type(job).__name__}, expected Job")
    if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id.strip():
        raise ScheduleError(f"item {position} needs a non-empty string id, got {job.id!r}")
    if job.id in seen:
        raise ScheduleError(f"duplicate job id {job.id!r} at positions {seen[job.id]} and {position}")
    if not isinstance(job.priority, int) or isinstance(job.priority, bool):
        raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} priority must be an int, got {job.priority!r}")


def _dependencies(job: Job, known: dict[str, int]) -> dict[str, None]:
    """Validate depends_on and return its names in order, without duplicates."""
    if isinstance(job.depends_on, str):
        raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends_on must be a collection of job IDs, not a string")
    try:
        names = tuple(job.depends_on)
    except TypeError:
        raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends_on must be iterable, got {job.depends_on!r}") from None
    for name in names:
        if not isinstance(name, str):
            raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} has a non-string dependency {name!r}")
        if name not in known:
            raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {name!r}")
    return dict.fromkeys(names)


def _find_cycle(deps: dict[str, dict[str, None]], blocked: list[str]) -> str:
    """Follow unscheduled dependencies from a blocked job until one repeats."""
    blocked_set = set(blocked)
    path = [blocked[0]]
    index = {blocked[0]: 0}
    while True:
        # Every blocked job has at least one blocked dependency, so this always finds one.
        node = next(name for name in deps[path[-1]] if name in blocked_set)
        if node in index:
            return " -> ".join(path[index[node]:] + [node])
        index[node] = len(path)
        path.append(node)


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order.

    Jobs run after all of their dependencies. Among ready jobs, higher priority
    goes first and equal priorities keep their input order.
    """
    job_list = list(jobs)
    positions: dict[str, int] = {}
    for position, job in enumerate(job_list):
        _check_job(job, position, positions)
        positions[job.id] = position

    deps = {job.id: _dependencies(job, positions) for job in job_list}
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {job.id: [] for job in job_list}
    for job_id, names in deps.items():
        for name in names:
            dependents[name].append(job_id)

    priority = {job.id: job.priority for job in job_list}
    waiting = {job_id: len(names) for job_id, names in deps.items()}
    ready = [
        (-priority[job_id], positions[job_id], job_id)
        for job_id, count in waiting.items()
        if count == 0
    ]
    heapify(ready)

    plan: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        _, _, job_id = heappop(ready)
        plan.append(job_id)
        for child in dependents[job_id]:
            waiting[child] -= 1
            if waiting[child] == 0:
                heappush(ready, (-priority[child], positions[child], child))

    if len(plan) < len(job_list):
        done = set(plan)
        blocked = [job.id for job in job_list if job.id not in done]
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"dependency cycle: {_find_cycle(deps, blocked)}; "
            f"unschedulable jobs: {', '.join(blocked)}"
        )
    return plan

haiku-5.5/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import random
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


def _reference_order(jobs):
    """Brute-force oracle: at each step pick the best ready job by (-priority, input index)."""
    index = {job.id: i for i, job in enumerate(jobs)}
    done = []
    while len(done) < len(jobs):
        ready = [
            job for job in jobs
            if job.id not in done and all(dep in done for dep in job.depends_on)
        ]
        best = min(ready, key=lambda job: (-job.priority, index[job.id]))
        done.append(best.id)
    return done


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    # Ordering

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_accepts_generator_input(self):
        jobs = (job for job in [Job("b", depends_on=("a",)), Job("a")])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_newly_ready_high_priority_job_beats_waiting_low_priority_job(self):
        jobs = [Job("gate", 5), Job("low", 1), Job("high", 9, depends_on=("gate",))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["gate", "high", "low"])

    def test_equal_priority_released_jobs_keep_input_order_not_alphabetical(self):
        jobs = [Job("root"), Job("z", depends_on=("root",)), Job("a", depends_on=("root",))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "z", "a"])

    def test_diamond_runs_shared_parent_first_and_join_last(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("join", 0, ("left", "right")),
            Job("right", 1, ("top",)),
            Job("left", 2, ("top",)),
            Job("top", 0),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["top", "left", "right", "join"])

    def test_duplicate_dependency_names_are_harmless(self):
        jobs = [Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=("a", "a"))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_list_valued_depends_on_is_accepted(self):
        jobs = [Job("b", depends_on=["a"]), Job("a")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_random_dags_match_reference_and_respect_dependencies(self):
        rng = random.Random(1234)
        for trial in range(25):
            with self.subTest(trial=trial):
                count = rng.randint(1, 60)
                ids = [f"job{i}" for i in range(count)]
                rng.shuffle(ids)
                jobs = []
                for i, job_id in enumerate(ids):
                    # Depend only on earlier entries in the shuffled list, so the graph is acyclic.
                    candidates = ids[:i]
                    deps = tuple(rng.sample(candidates, rng.randint(0, min(3, len(candidates)))))
                    jobs.append(Job(job_id, rng.randint(-3, 3), deps))

                plan = plan_jobs(jobs)

                self.assertEqual(sorted(plan), sorted(ids))
                self.assertEqual(len(plan), len(set(plan)))
                position = {job_id: n for n, job_id in enumerate(plan)}
                for job in jobs:
                    for dep in job.depends_on:
                        self.assertLess(position[dep], position[job.id])
                self.assertEqual(plan, _reference_order(jobs))

    # Input validation

    def test_duplicate_ids_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "duplicate job id 'a'"):
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("a", priority=1)])

    def test_empty_or_blank_ids_are_rejected(self):
        for bad_id in ("", "   "):
            with self.subTest(bad_id=bad_id):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "non-empty string id"):
                    plan_jobs([Job(bad_id)])

    def test_non_string_ids_are_rejected(self):
        for bad_id in (None, 3, ["a"]):
            with self.subTest(bad_id=bad_id):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "non-empty string id"):
                    plan_jobs([Job(bad_id)])

    def test_unknown_dependency_message_names_the_missing_job(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "'build' depends on unknown job 'missing'"):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_non_string_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        for bad_dep in (None, 0, ["a"]):
            with self.subTest(bad_dep=bad_dep):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "non-string dependency"):
                    plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=(bad_dep,))])

    def test_bare_string_depends_on_is_rejected(self):
        # Without this check "fetch" would be read as the characters "f", "e", ...
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "not a string"):
            plan_jobs([Job("fetch"), Job("build", depends_on="fetch")])

    def test_non_iterable_depends_on_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "must be iterable"):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=None)])

    def test_non_job_items_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "expected Job"):
            plan_jobs(["a"])

    def test_priority_must_be_a_plain_int(self):
        # bool is a subclass of int, but True as a priority is almost always a mistake.
        for bad_priority in (1.5, "high", True):
            with self.subTest(priority=bad_priority):
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "priority must be an int"):
                    plan_jobs([Job("a", priority=bad_priority)])

    # Cycles

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "dependency cycle: a -> a"):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])

    def test_two_job_cycle_reports_its_ids(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "dependency cycle: a -> b -> a"):
            plan_jobs(jobs)

    def test_cycle_is_detected_alongside_acyclic_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("setup"),
            Job("x", depends_on=("y",)),
            Job("y", depends_on=("z",)),
            Job("z", depends_on=("x",)),
            Job("after", depends_on=("setup",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "dependency cycle: x -> y -> z -> x"):
            plan_jobs(jobs)

    def test_cycle_message_names_the_loop_even_when_it_starts_downstream(self):
        jobs = [Job("c", depends_on=("a",)), Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "dependency cycle: a -> b -> a"):
            plan_jobs(jobs)

    def test_jobs_blocked_by_a_cycle_are_listed_as_unschedulable(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",)), Job("c", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ScheduleError, "unschedulable jobs: a, b, c"):
            plan_jobs(jobs)

    # Immutability

    def test_does_not_mutate_inputs(self):
        needs_a = ["a"]
        jobs = [Job("c", 1, ("b",)), Job("b", 0, needs_a), Job("a", 2)]
        before = list(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])
        self.assertEqual(jobs, before)
        self.assertEqual(needs_a, ["a"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

glm-5.3/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Task 01 — Response: dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs in jobqueue.py is implemented with stdlib only (heapq, dataclasses, typing) in three phases:

  1. Validation. The input iterable is materialized into a private list (never mutating the caller's collection). Each item's id must be a non-empty string and unique; duplicates and malformed ids raise ScheduleError with the offending id and index. Dependencies must be strings naming supplied jobs; unknown or non-string dependencies raise ScheduleError naming both the dependent job and the bad dependency. Duplicate entries in a single depends_on are de-duplicated so a job still becomes ready (indegrees count unique dependencies). Non-iterable jobs / depends_on values are also converted to ScheduleError.
  2. Scheduling. Kahn's algorithm with a (-priority, index) min-heap. Only currently-ready jobs enter the heap, so the pop order is exactly "highest priority first, ties broken by original input order", while dependencies always precede dependents. Complexity is O(V + E log V).
  3. Cycle detection. If scheduling stalls with jobs left over, a walk through the unscheduled subgraph follows unscheduled dependencies until it revisits a node, yielding a concrete cycle for the diagnostic, e.g. dependency cycle detected: a -> b -> a (2 job(s) unschedulable). Self dependencies are caught by the same path.

No job, depends_on list, or input iterable is ever mutated (the module only reads them), and empty input returns [].

Edge cases covered in test_jobqueue.py

Empty input (list/tuple/generator), single job, 500-node chain, diamond dependencies, priorities applied at readiness time (late-arriving high-priority job jumps ahead of an already-ready low-priority one), input-order tie-breaks, negative priorities, interleaving of disconnected components, dependency order beating priority, duplicate ids, empty and non-string ids, non-Job items, non-iterable input, unknown dependency message content, non-string dependencies, duplicate dependencies counted once, self-cycle, two-node cycle, cycle with blocked downstream jobs (IDs in message), non-mutation of jobs/depends_on/input order, determinism across repeated calls, generator input, payload ignored, a realistic fetch→lint/test→package→deploy pipeline, and 50 seeded random DAGs checked with a generic topological-invariant helper.

Verification

Command (run inside 01-feature-building/):

python3 -m unittest -v test_jobqueue

Result:

glm-5.3/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

glm-5.3/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

import heapq

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def _find_cycle(
    items: list[Job],
    dep_indices: list[list[int]],
    emitted: list[bool],
) -> list[str]:
    """Return the IDs of one dependency cycle among the unscheduled jobs.

    Every unscheduled job still has at least one unscheduled dependency
    (otherwise Kahn's algorithm would have scheduled it), so repeatedly
    following unscheduled dependencies must eventually revisit a job,
    which closes a cycle.
    """
    leftover = [i for i in range(len(items)) if not emitted[i]]
    deps_of = {
        items[i].id: [items[d].id for d in dep_indices[i] if not emitted[d]]
        for i in leftover
    }
    start = items[leftover[0]].id
    seen = {start}
    path = [start]
    current = start
    while True:
        current = deps_of[current][0]
        if current in seen:
            return path[path.index(current):] + [current]
        path.append(current)
        seen.add(current)


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid, deterministic execution order.

    Every job appears exactly once and never before any of its
    dependencies.  Among the jobs that are currently ready, higher
    ``priority`` is scheduled first; equal priorities fall back to
    original input order.

    Raises :class:`ScheduleError` for invalid input: ids that are not
    non-empty strings, duplicate ids, dependencies naming jobs that were
    not supplied, non-string dependencies, and dependency cycles.  The
    supplied iterable, the jobs, and their dependency lists are never
    mutated.  Empty input yields an empty list.
    """
    try:
        items = list(jobs)
    except TypeError:
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"jobs must be an iterable of Job objects, got {type(jobs).__name__}"
        ) from None

    count = len(items)
    if count == 0:
        return []

    index_by_id: dict = {}
    for i, job in enumerate(items):
        job_id = getattr(job, "id", None)
        if not isinstance(job_id, str) or not job_id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job at index {i} has invalid id {job_id!r}: "
                "ids must be non-empty strings"
            )
        if job_id in index_by_id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"duplicate job id {job_id!r} at indices "
                f"{index_by_id[job_id]} and {i}"
            )
        index_by_id[job_id] = i

    dep_indices = []
    for job in items:
        raw = getattr(job, "depends_on", ())
        try:
            deps = list(raw)
        except TypeError:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job {job.id!r} has non-iterable depends_on {raw!r}"
            ) from None
        resolved = []
        seen = set()
        for dep in deps:
            if not isinstance(dep, str):
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job.id!r} has non-string dependency {dep!r}"
                )
            if dep in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(dep)
            if dep not in index_by_id:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}"
                )
            resolved.append(index_by_id[dep])
        dep_indices.append(resolved)

    indegree = [len(deps) for deps in dep_indices]
    dependents = [[] for _ in range(count)]
    for i, deps in enumerate(dep_indices):
        for dep in deps:
            dependents[dep].append(i)

    ready = [
        (-getattr(job, "priority", 0), i)
        for i, job in enumerate(items)
        if indegree[i] == 0
    ]
    heapq.heapify(ready)

    order = []
    emitted = [False] * count
    while ready:
        _, i = heapq.heappop(ready)
        emitted[i] = True
        order.append(items[i].id)
        for dependent in dependents[i]:
            indegree[dependent] -= 1
            if indegree[dependent] == 0:
                heapq.heappush(
                    ready,
                    (-getattr(items[dependent], "priority", 0), dependent),
                )

    if len(order) != count:
        cycle = _find_cycle(items, dep_indices, emitted)
        unscheduled = count - len(order)
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"dependency cycle detected: {' -> '.join(cycle)} "
            f"({unscheduled} job(s) unschedulable)"
        )

    return order

glm-5.3/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import random
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


def assert_valid_plan(testcase, jobs, result):
    """Assert generic topological invariants of ``result`` for ``jobs``."""
    ids = [job.id for job in jobs]
    testcase.assertEqual(
        sorted(result), sorted(ids), "every job id must appear exactly once"
    )
    position = {job_id: index for index, job_id in enumerate(result)}
    for job in jobs:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            testcase.assertGreater(
                position[job.id],
                position[dep],
                msg=f"{dep!r} must run before {job.id!r}",
            )


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(()), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(iter([])), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only", priority=7)]), ["only"])

    def test_long_chain(self):
        jobs = [
            Job(f"j{i}", depends_on=(f"j{i - 1}",) if i else ())
            for i in range(500)
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [f"j{i}" for i in range(500)])

    def test_diamond_dependency(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("d", depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("b", priority=-1, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", priority=1, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a"),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        assert_valid_plan(self, jobs, result)
        self.assertEqual(result, ["a", "c", "b", "d"])

    def test_priority_applies_when_job_becomes_ready(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("low", priority=0),
            Job("gate", priority=1),
            Job("high", priority=10, depends_on=("gate",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["gate", "high", "low"])

    def test_equal_priorities_fall_back_to_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 5), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 5)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_negative_priorities(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", -3), Job("b", -1), Job("c", -2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "c", "a"])

    def test_disconnected_components_interleave_by_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a1", 1),
            Job("b1", 5),
            Job("a2", 0, depends_on=("a1",)),
            Job("b2", 0, depends_on=("b1",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b1", "a1", "a2", "b2"])

    def test_equal_priority_dependent_waits_despite_earlier_index(self):
        jobs = [Job("b", 2, depends_on=("a",)), Job("a", 2), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_duplicate_ids_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b"), Job("a")])
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("duplicate", message)
        self.assertIn("'a'", message)

    def test_empty_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("ok"), Job("")])
        self.assertIn("index 1", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_non_string_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("ok"), Job(42)])

    def test_non_job_item_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("ok"), "not-a-job"])

    def test_non_iterable_input_is_rejected(self):
        for bad in (None, 42, Job("x")):
            with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
                plan_jobs(bad)

    def test_unknown_dependency_message_names_both_jobs(self):
        jobs = [Job("build", depends_on=("ghost",)), Job("other")]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("build", message)
        self.assertIn("ghost", message)

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle", message.lower())
        self.assertIn("a", message)

    def test_two_node_cycle_message_lists_ids(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", message)
        self.assertIn("b", message)

    def test_cycle_with_blocked_downstream_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("clean"),
            Job("compile", depends_on=("test",)),
            Job("test", depends_on=("compile",)),
            Job("deploy", depends_on=("test",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("compile", message)
        self.assertIn("test", message)

    def test_does_not_mutate_jobs_or_dependency_lists(self):
        depends = ["a", "a"]
        jobs = [Job("b", 3, depends_on=depends), Job("a", 9)]
        depends_before = list(depends)
        ids_before = [job.id for job in jobs]

        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

        self.assertEqual(depends, depends_before)
        self.assertEqual(jobs[0].depends_on, depends_before)
        self.assertEqual([job.id for job in jobs], ids_before)

    def test_repeated_calls_are_deterministic(self):
        jobs = (Job("b", depends_on=("a",)), Job("a"))
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), plan_jobs(jobs))

    def test_generator_input(self):
        jobs = [Job("b", 1, depends_on=("a",)), Job("a", 2), Job("c", 0)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(iter(jobs)), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_duplicate_dependencies_count_once(self):
        jobs = [Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=("a", "a", "a"))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_non_string_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=(123,))])
        self.assertIn("b", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_payload_is_ignored_for_ordering(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", payload={"k": 1}),
            Job("b", payload=None, depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_pipeline_example(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("deploy", depends_on=("package",), priority=10),
            Job("package", depends_on=("lint", "test"), priority=5),
            Job("test", depends_on=("fetch",), priority=3),
            Job("lint", depends_on=("fetch",), priority=4),
            Job("fetch", priority=2),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        assert_valid_plan(self, jobs, result)
        self.assertEqual(
            result, ["fetch", "lint", "test", "package", "deploy"]
        )

    def test_random_dags_stay_valid_and_deterministic(self):
        rng = random.Random(20261011)
        for _ in range(50):
            n = rng.randint(0, 40)
            jobs = []
            for i in range(n):
                deps = tuple(
                    f"j{d}"
                    for d in rng.sample(
                        range(i), min(i, rng.randint(0, 3))
                    )
                )
                jobs.append(Job(f"j{i}", rng.randint(-10, 10), deps))
            result = plan_jobs(jobs)
            assert_valid_plan(self, jobs, result)
            self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), result)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

hy4-preview-codebuddy/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Task 01 — dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs(jobs) in jobqueue.py runs Kahn's algorithm over the dependency graph, with a heap as the "ready" frontier so selection is deterministic.

  1. Materialize and validate. The input iterable is consumed once into a list. Each entry must be a Job; each id must be a non-empty string and unique. Input position is recorded in position, priority in priority.
  2. Normalize dependencies. _normalize_dependencies copies each depends_on into a fresh list (never touching the caller's object), rejects a bare string (a common depends_on="a" mistake), rejects empty/non-string dependency names, rejects self-dependencies, and de-duplicates repeated entries so a dependency listed twice cannot double-decrement the indegree.
  3. Build edges. Each dependency must exist in position, otherwise ScheduleError: job 'build' depends on unknown job 'ghost'. Forward edges dependents[dep] -> job and indegree[job] are recorded.
  4. Schedule. All indegree-0 jobs are pushed as (-priority, position, id) tuples. The heap yields the highest priority first; equal priorities fall back to input position. When a job is emitted, its dependents' indegrees are decremented and any job reaching zero is pushed. Dependencies therefore always win over priority — a high-priority blocked job never stalls ready work.
  5. Cycle detection. If the plan is shorter than the job count, the leftover jobs are exactly those on or downstream of a cycle. _describe_cycle walks them with an iterative DFS (no recursion limit risk) starting from the lowest input position and returns one concrete cycle, e.g. dependency cycle detected involving jobs: a -> b -> a.

Complexity: O(V + E) plus O(V log V) heap operations. Only the standard library is used (heapq, dataclasses, typing); Job itself is unchanged.

Edge cases covered (29 tests in test_jobqueue.py)

  • Ordering: dependencies beat priority; priority-then-input-order ties; negative priorities; blocked high-priority job does not stall ready jobs; chains; diamond/shared-edge graphs; a 6-job graph property-checked for "each ID once, always after its dependencies"; determinism across repeat calls; generators accepted as input; payload ignored.
  • Validation: unknown dependency (message names both jobs); duplicate job IDs; empty and non-string job IDs; empty dependency name; bare-string depends_on; non-Job entries; self-dependency.
  • Cycles: two-job cycle, three-job cycle (all members named), and a cycle hanging off an acyclic prefix (only the cycle members appear in the diagnostic); an acyclic graph with shared edges is not misreported.
  • Immutability: input Jobs deep-compared before/after; input list order unchanged; a list-valued depends_on is accepted and left untouched.

Verification

python3 -m unittest -v test_jobqueue.py

Result: Ran 29 tests in 0.002s — OK (all 29 pass; the same suite also passes via python3 -m unittest discover -s . -p 'test_*.py'). Python 3, standard library only, no network access used.

hy4-preview-codebuddy/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

hy4-preview-codebuddy/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable
import heapq


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def _normalize_dependencies(job: Job) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """Return a job's dependencies as a duplicate-free tuple, never mutating input."""
    raw = job.depends_on
    if raw is None:
        return ()
    if isinstance(raw, str):
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"job {job.id!r} lists a string as depends_on; "
            "pass a sequence of job IDs (e.g. ('other',)), not a bare id"
        )
    try:
        candidates = list(raw)
    except TypeError:
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"job {job.id!r} has a non-iterable depends_on value: {raw!r}"
        ) from None

    seen: set[str] = set()
    unique: list[str] = []
    for dep in candidates:
        if not isinstance(dep, str) or not dep:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job {job.id!r} has an invalid dependency {dep!r}: "
                "dependencies must be non-empty strings"
            )
        if dep == job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends on itself")
        if dep not in seen:
            seen.add(dep)
            unique.append(dep)
    return tuple(unique)


def _describe_cycle(
    remaining: set[str],
    deps: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]],
    position: dict[str, int],
) -> list[str]:
    """Return one concrete cycle (as a list of IDs) among the unresolved jobs."""
    by_input_order = sorted(remaining, key=lambda job_id: position[job_id])
    state: dict[str, int] = {}  # 1 = on current path, 2 = fully explored

    for start in by_input_order:
        if start in state:
            continue
        state[start] = 1
        path = [start]
        stack = [(start, iter(deps[start]))]
        while stack:
            node, dep_iter = stack[-1]
            descended = False
            for dep in dep_iter:
                if dep not in remaining:
                    continue
                if state.get(dep) == 1:
                    return path[path.index(dep):]
                if dep not in state:
                    state[dep] = 1
                    path.append(dep)
                    stack.append((dep, iter(deps[dep])))
                    descended = True
                    break
            if not descended:
                state[node] = 2
                path.pop()
                stack.pop()
    return by_input_order


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order."""
    ordered_jobs = list(jobs)

    position: dict[str, int] = {}
    priority: dict[str, int] = {}
    for pos, job in enumerate(ordered_jobs):
        if not isinstance(job, Job):
            raise ScheduleError(f"jobs[{pos}] is not a Job instance: {job!r}")
        job_id = job.id
        if not isinstance(job_id, str) or not job_id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"jobs[{pos}] has an invalid id {job_id!r}: ids must be non-empty strings"
            )
        if job_id in position:
            raise ScheduleError(f"duplicate job id {job_id!r}")
        position[job_id] = pos
        priority[job_id] = job.priority

    deps: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {}
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {job_id: [] for job_id in position}
    indegree: dict[str, int] = {}

    for job in ordered_jobs:
        job_id = job.id
        job_deps = _normalize_dependencies(job)
        for dep in job_deps:
            if dep not in position:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job_id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}"
                )
            dependents[dep].append(job_id)
        deps[job_id] = job_deps
        indegree[job_id] = len(job_deps)

    # (-priority, input position, id) keeps the highest priority first and
    # breaks ties by original input order.
    ready: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = [
        (-priority[job.id], position[job.id], job.id)
        for job in ordered_jobs
        if indegree[job.id] == 0
    ]
    heapq.heapify(ready)

    plan: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(ready)
        plan.append(job_id)
        for dependent in dependents[job_id]:
            indegree[dependent] -= 1
            if indegree[dependent] == 0:
                heapq.heappush(
                    ready, (-priority[dependent], position[dependent], dependent)
                )

    if len(plan) != len(position):
        unresolved = {job_id for job_id in position if indegree[job_id] > 0}
        cycle = _describe_cycle(unresolved, deps, position)
        raise ScheduleError(
            "dependency cycle detected involving jobs: "
            + " -> ".join([*cycle, cycle[0]])
        )

    return plan

hy4-preview-codebuddy/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import copy
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    # --- basic shapes -----------------------------------------------------

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_single_job_without_dependencies(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])

    def test_chain_of_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("c", priority=99, depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", priority=50, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_diamond_graph(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("join", depends_on=("left", "right")),
            Job("left", depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("right", depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("root"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "left", "right", "join"])

    def test_blocked_high_priority_job_does_not_stall_ready_ones(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("late", priority=10, depends_on=("slow",)),
            Job("ready_low", priority=1),
            Job("slow", priority=0),
            Job("ready_high", priority=2),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(
            plan_jobs(jobs), ["ready_high", "ready_low", "slow", "late"]
        )

    def test_negative_priorities_order_descending(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", -5), Job("b", -1), Job("c", -3)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "c", "a"])

    def test_duplicate_dependencies_are_tolerated(self):
        jobs = [Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=("a", "a"))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_payload_is_ignored_for_ordering(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", payload={"x": 1}), Job("b", 1, payload=[1, 2])]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a"])

    def test_accepts_any_iterable(self):
        jobs = iter([Job("a", 3), Job("b", 3)])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_result_is_deterministic_across_calls(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 1), Job("b", 1), Job("c", 2, depends_on=("a",))]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), plan_jobs(jobs))

    def test_complex_graph_yields_each_job_once_and_after_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("lint", priority=7, depends_on=("parse",)),
            Job("parse", priority=1, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=3),
            Job("test", priority=9, depends_on=("lint", "parse")),
            Job("report", priority=0, depends_on=("test", "lint", "fetch")),
            Job("docs", priority=100),
        ]
        plan = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(sorted(plan), sorted(j.id for j in jobs))
        self.assertEqual(len(plan), len(set(plan)))
        seen: set[str] = set()
        for job_id in plan:
            for job in jobs:
                if job.id == job_id:
                    self.assertTrue(seen.issuperset(job.depends_on))
            seen.add(job_id)
        self.assertEqual(plan[0], "docs")  # highest priority among ready jobs

    # --- validation errors ------------------------------------------------

    def test_self_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("loop", depends_on=("loop",))])
        self.assertIn("loop", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_duplicate_job_ids_are_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("dup"), Job("dup", priority=5)])
        self.assertIn("dup", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_empty_job_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_non_string_job_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(3)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_empty_dependency_name_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=("",))])
        self.assertIn("b", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_bare_string_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on="a")])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_unknown_dependency_message_names_both_jobs(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("ghost",))])
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("build", message)
        self.assertIn("ghost", message)

    def test_non_job_entry_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), "not-a-job"])  # type: ignore[list-item]

    # --- cycles -----------------------------------------------------------

    def test_two_job_cycle_is_rejected(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", message)
        self.assertIn("b", message)

    def test_three_job_cycle_names_every_member(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("x", depends_on=("z",)),
            Job("y", depends_on=("x",)),
            Job("z", depends_on=("y",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        for job_id in ("x", "y", "z"):
            self.assertIn(job_id, message)

    def test_cycle_reachable_only_through_acyclic_prefix(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("root"),
            Job("m", depends_on=("root", "n")),
            Job("n", depends_on=("m",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("m", message)
        self.assertIn("n", message)
        self.assertNotIn("root", message)

    def test_acyclic_graph_with_shared_edges_is_not_a_cycle(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a"),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a", "b")),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    # --- immutability -----------------------------------------------------

    def test_input_jobs_are_not_mutated(self):
        deps = ("fetch",)
        jobs = [Job("build", priority=2, depends_on=deps), Job("fetch")]
        snapshot = copy.deepcopy(jobs)
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(jobs, snapshot)
        self.assertEqual(jobs[0].depends_on, ("fetch",))

    def test_input_sequence_is_not_mutated(self):
        jobs = [Job("b", 5), Job("a", 5)]
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual([j.id for j in jobs], ["b", "a"])

    def test_list_dependencies_are_accepted_and_not_mutated(self):
        deps = ["a"]
        jobs = [Job("a"), Job("b", depends_on=deps)]  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])
        self.assertEqual(deps, ["a"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
