gemini-3.8-flash/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

gemini-3.8-flash/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from collections.abc import Iterable
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import heapq
import math
from typing import Any


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def _find_cycle(remaining_ids: set[str], jobs_by_id: dict[str, Job]) -> list[str] | None:
    """Find a directed dependency cycle among remaining unscheduled jobs."""
    state: dict[str, int] = {job_id: 0 for job_id in remaining_ids}
    path: list[str] = []

    def dfs(node: str) -> list[str] | None:
        state[node] = 1
        path.append(node)
        for dep in jobs_by_id[node].depends_on:
            if dep not in remaining_ids:
                continue
            if state[dep] == 1:
                cycle_start = path.index(dep)
                return path[cycle_start:] + [dep]
            if state[dep] == 0:
                cycle = dfs(dep)
                if cycle is not None:
                    return cycle
        path.pop()
        state[node] = 2
        return None

    for start_node in sorted(remaining_ids):
        if state[start_node] == 0:
            cycle = dfs(start_node)
            if cycle is not None:
                return cycle
    return None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order.

    Jobs are executed after all their dependencies have run.
    Among currently ready jobs, higher priority runs first;
    ties are broken by original input order.
    """
    if jobs is None or not isinstance(jobs, Iterable):
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"jobs must be an iterable of Job instances, got {type(jobs).__name__}"
        )

    job_list = list(jobs)
    if not job_list:
        return []

    jobs_by_id: dict[str, Job] = {}
    job_indices: dict[str, int] = {}

    for index, job in enumerate(job_list):
        if not isinstance(job, Job) and not (
            hasattr(job, "id") and hasattr(job, "priority") and hasattr(job, "depends_on")
        ):
            raise ScheduleError(f"Expected Job instance, got {type(job).__name__}")

        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Job id must be a non-empty string, got {job.id!r}"
            )

        if job.id in jobs_by_id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"Duplicate job ID: {job.id!r}")

        if (
            isinstance(job.priority, bool)
            or not isinstance(job.priority, (int, float))
            or (isinstance(job.priority, float) and math.isnan(job.priority))
        ):
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Job {job.id!r} has invalid priority: {job.priority!r}"
            )

        if (
            job.depends_on is None
            or isinstance(job.depends_on, (str, bytes))
            or not isinstance(job.depends_on, Iterable)
        ):
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Job {job.id!r} depends_on must be an iterable of job ID strings"
            )

        jobs_by_id[job.id] = job
        job_indices[job.id] = index

    # Validate all dependencies exist and are valid non-empty strings
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {job.id: [] for job in job_list}
    in_degree: dict[str, int] = {job.id: 0 for job in job_list}

    for job in job_list:
        unique_deps: set[str] = set()
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            if not isinstance(dep, str) or not dep:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} has invalid dependency ID: {dep!r} (must be a non-empty string)"
                )
            if dep not in jobs_by_id:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"Job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}"
                )
            unique_deps.add(dep)

        in_degree[job.id] = len(unique_deps)
        for dep in unique_deps:
            dependents[dep].append(job.id)

    # Kahn's algorithm using priority queue
    # Heap stores: (-priority, original_index, job_id)
    ready_heap: list[tuple[float | int, int, str]] = []
    for index, job in enumerate(job_list):
        if in_degree[job.id] == 0:
            heapq.heappush(ready_heap, (-job.priority, index, job.id))

    scheduled: list[str] = []
    while ready_heap:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(ready_heap)
        scheduled.append(job_id)

        for succ_id in dependents[job_id]:
            in_degree[succ_id] -= 1
            if in_degree[succ_id] == 0:
                succ_job = jobs_by_id[succ_id]
                heapq.heappush(
                    ready_heap,
                    (-succ_job.priority, job_indices[succ_id], succ_id),
                )

    if len(scheduled) < len(job_list):
        remaining_ids = set(jobs_by_id.keys()) - set(scheduled)
        cycle = _find_cycle(remaining_ids, jobs_by_id)
        if cycle:
            cycle_nodes = sorted(set(cycle))
            cycle_str = " -> ".join(cycle)
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Dependency cycle detected involving jobs {cycle_nodes}: {cycle_str}"
            )
        else:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"Dependency cycle detected involving jobs: {sorted(remaining_ids)}"
            )

    return scheduled

gemini-3.8-flash/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import math
import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])
        self.assertIn("missing", str(cm.exception))

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(()), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(iter([])), [])

    def test_single_job_without_dependencies(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("solo", priority=42)]), ["solo"])

    def test_linear_chain_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("deploy", priority=50, depends_on=("test",)),
            Job("test", priority=40, depends_on=("build",)),
            Job("build", priority=30, depends_on=("lint",)),
            Job("lint", priority=20),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["lint", "build", "test", "deploy"])

    def test_diamond_dependencies(self):
        # A is base; B (pri 5) and C (pri 10) depend on A; D depends on B and C.
        jobs = [
            Job("d", priority=100, depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("b", priority=5, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", priority=10, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a", priority=1),
        ]
        # 'a' must run first; then 'c' has higher priority than 'b'; finally 'd'.
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "c", "b", "d"])

    def test_disconnected_components_respect_priorities(self):
        # Component 1: root1 -> child1
        # Component 2: root2 -> child2
        jobs = [
            Job("root1", priority=10),
            Job("child1", priority=50, depends_on=("root1",)),
            Job("root2", priority=20),
            Job("child2", priority=60, depends_on=("root2",)),
        ]
        # At start, root2 (pri 20) > root1 (pri 10).
        # root2 finishes, unblocking child2 (pri 60).
        # Currently ready: child2 (pri 60), root1 (pri 10).
        # child2 runs first.
        # Then root1 runs, unblocking child1 (pri 50).
        # Finally child1 runs.
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root2", "child2", "root1", "child1"])

    def test_negative_and_zero_priorities(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("low", priority=-10),
            Job("zero", priority=0),
            Job("mid", priority=-2),
            Job("high", priority=5),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["high", "zero", "mid", "low"])

    def test_dynamic_readiness_priority_selection(self):
        # Ready initially: slow_low (pri 1), idle_wait (pri 5).
        # idle_wait should run before slow_low, but once slow_low finishes,
        # it unblocks urgent (pri 100), which should run ahead of idle_wait2 (pri 10).
        jobs = [
            Job("idle_wait", priority=5),
            Job("slow_low", priority=1),
            Job("idle_wait2", priority=10),
            Job("urgent", priority=100, depends_on=("slow_low",)),
        ]
        # Step 1: ready are idle_wait2 (10), idle_wait (5), slow_low (1).
        # Step 1 picks idle_wait2.
        # Step 2: ready are idle_wait (5), slow_low (1). Picks idle_wait.
        # Step 3: ready is slow_low (1). Picks slow_low.
        # Step 4: urgent (100) unblocked and picked.
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["idle_wait2", "idle_wait", "slow_low", "urgent"])

    def test_equal_priority_tie_breaking_by_input_order(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("job_3", priority=10),
            Job("job_1", priority=10),
            Job("job_2", priority=10),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["job_3", "job_1", "job_2"])

    def test_job_id_must_be_non_empty_string(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("", priority=1)])
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(123)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_job_id_must_be_unique(self):
        jobs = [Job("duplicate", priority=1), Job("duplicate", priority=2)]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("duplicate", str(cm.exception))

    def test_non_iterable_input_raises_schedule_error(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs(None)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs(12345)  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_non_job_items_raise_schedule_error(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs(["not_a_job"])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("valid"), None])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_invalid_priority_raises_schedule_error(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_pri", priority="high")])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_pri", priority=None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_pri", priority=True)])  # bool disallowed
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_pri", priority=float("nan"))])

    def test_invalid_depends_on_raises_schedule_error(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_dep", depends_on=None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_dep", depends_on="string_not_iterable_of_ids")])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_dep", depends_on=("",))])
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("bad_dep", depends_on=(123,))])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_duplicate_dependencies_in_single_job_handled_correctly(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("parent"),
            Job("child", depends_on=("parent", "parent")),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["parent", "child"])

    def test_input_is_not_mutated(self):
        dep_list = ["dep_a", "dep_b"]
        job_a = Job("dep_a")
        job_b = Job("dep_b")
        job_c = Job("consumer", depends_on=dep_list)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        jobs = [job_c, job_b, job_a]
        original_jobs = list(jobs)

        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(result, ["dep_b", "dep_a", "consumer"])
        # Original jobs list intact
        self.assertEqual(jobs, original_jobs)
        # Original dependency list intact
        self.assertEqual(dep_list, ["dep_a", "dep_b"])

    def test_generator_input_supported(self):
        def job_stream():
            yield Job("first", priority=1)
            yield Job("second", priority=2)

        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(job_stream()), ["second", "first"])

    def test_cycle_detection_self_dependency(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs([Job("self_dep", depends_on=("self_dep",))])
        self.assertIn("self_dep", str(cm.exception))

    def test_cycle_detection_two_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        err = str(cm.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", err)
        self.assertIn("b", err)

    def test_cycle_detection_three_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("step1", depends_on=("step3",)),
            Job("step2", depends_on=("step1",)),
            Job("step3", depends_on=("step2",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        err = str(cm.exception)
        self.assertIn("step1", err)
        self.assertIn("step2", err)
        self.assertIn("step3", err)

    def test_cycle_detection_with_independent_ready_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("ready1", priority=10),
            Job("ready2", priority=5),
            Job("cycle_a", depends_on=("cycle_b",)),
            Job("cycle_b", depends_on=("cycle_a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        err = str(cm.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle_a", err)
        self.assertIn("cycle_b", err)

    def test_cycle_detection_downstream_dependency(self):
        # cycle_a <-> cycle_b; downstream depends on cycle_b.
        jobs = [
            Job("cycle_a", depends_on=("cycle_b",)),
            Job("cycle_b", depends_on=("cycle_a",)),
            Job("downstream", depends_on=("cycle_b",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        err = str(cm.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle_a", err)
        self.assertIn("cycle_b", err)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

kimi-k3/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Task 01 — Dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs in jobqueue.py is implemented in three phases using only the Python standard library (heapq, dataclasses, typing):

  1. Validation — the input iterable is materialized once into a list. Each job ID must be a non-empty string and unique; every entry in depends_on must reference a supplied job. Any violation raises ScheduleError with a message naming the offending ID(s).
  2. Graph construction — indegree counts and dependent adjacency lists are built in fresh dicts keyed by job ID, so the supplied Job objects and their dependency tuples are never mutated.
  3. Kahn's algorithm with a priority heap — ready jobs live in a min-heap keyed by (-priority, input_index, job_id), so among ready jobs the highest priority is always emitted first, with equal priorities broken by original input order (deterministic). If the emitted count is smaller than the input count, the leftover jobs form one or more cycles; a ScheduleError is raised listing the remaining (cycle-involved) job IDs.

Complexity: O(V + E log V) — heap operations dominate.

Edge cases covered by tests (18 total)

  • Empty input returns []; single job; arbitrary iterable (generator) input.
  • Invalid IDs: empty string, non-string ID, duplicate IDs (error names the ID).
  • Unknown dependency rejected.
  • Cycles: self-dependency, two-node cycle, a 3-node cycle mixed with acyclic jobs — in each case the error message includes the involved IDs.
  • Ordering: dependencies override priority; priority applies only among currently-ready jobs; equal priorities fall back to input order; negative priorities order correctly.
  • Structural: diamond dependency (each job emitted exactly once, after all deps), repeated dependency edges, a 200-job chain.
  • Non-mutation: input jobs and their dependency tuples are unchanged and the same objects after the call.

Verification

Command (run inside this directory):

python3 -m unittest test_jobqueue -v

Result:

Ran 18 tests in 0.005s

OK

kimi-k3/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

kimi-k3/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

import heapq
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order.

    Every job appears exactly once, after all of its dependencies. Among the
    jobs that are ready at any point, higher ``priority`` wins; ties are
    broken by original input order. Raises :class:`ScheduleError` for
    invalid input (empty/duplicate IDs, unknown dependencies) or cycles.
    The input jobs are never mutated.
    """
    job_list = list(jobs)

    by_id: dict[str, Job] = {}
    for job in job_list:
        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"job id must be a non-empty string, got {job.id!r}")
        if job.id in by_id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"duplicate job id: {job.id!r}")
        by_id[job.id] = job

    # Build indegree counts and dependent lists from copies; never touch input.
    indegree: dict[str, int] = {job.id: 0 for job in job_list}
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {job.id: [] for job in job_list}
    for job in job_list:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            if dep not in by_id:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}"
                )
            indegree[job.id] += 1
            dependents[dep].append(job.id)

    # Kahn's algorithm; heap entries are (-priority, input_index, job_id) so
    # the ready job with the highest priority (then earliest input order) is
    # always chosen next.
    input_index = {job.id: index for index, job in enumerate(job_list)}
    ready: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = [
        (-job.priority, input_index[job.id], job.id)
        for job in job_list
        if indegree[job.id] == 0
    ]
    heapq.heapify(ready)

    order: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(ready)
        order.append(job_id)
        for dependent in dependents[job_id]:
            indegree[dependent] -= 1
            if indegree[dependent] == 0:
                dep_job = by_id[dependent]
                heapq.heappush(
                    ready, (-dep_job.priority, input_index[dependent], dependent)
                )

    if len(order) != len(job_list):
        remaining = sorted(jid for jid, deg in indegree.items() if deg > 0)
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"dependency cycle detected involving jobs: {', '.join(remaining)}"
        )

    return order

kimi-k3/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only", priority=-3)]), ["only"])

    def test_duplicate_id_is_rejected(self):
        jobs = [Job("a"), Job("a", priority=9)]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_empty_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_non_string_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(42)])

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_two_node_cycle_is_detected(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", message)
        self.assertIn("b", message)

    def test_cycle_among_acyclic_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("ok"),
            Job("x", depends_on=("z",)),
            Job("y", depends_on=("x",)),
            Job("z", depends_on=("y",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        for jid in ("x", "y", "z"):
            self.assertIn(jid, message)

    def test_diamond_dependency_runs_once_after_all_deps(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("top", priority=50, depends_on=("left", "right")),
            Job("left", depends_on=("base",)),
            Job("right", depends_on=("base",)),
            Job("base"),
        ]
        order = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(sorted(order), ["base", "left", "right", "top"])
        self.assertLess(order.index("base"), order.index("left"))
        self.assertLess(order.index("base"), order.index("right"))
        self.assertLess(order.index("left"), order.index("top"))
        self.assertLess(order.index("right"), order.index("top"))

    def test_priority_only_applies_among_ready_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("low-but-free", priority=1),
            Job("high-blocked", priority=100, depends_on=("low-but-free",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["low-but-free", "high-blocked"])

    def test_negative_priorities_order_correctly(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", -5), Job("b", -10), Job("c", 0)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["c", "a", "b"])

    def test_input_is_not_mutated(self):
        deps = ("base",)
        jobs = [
            Job("top", depends_on=deps),
            Job("base"),
        ]
        snapshot = [(j.id, j.priority, j.depends_on) for j in jobs]
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual([(j.id, j.priority, j.depends_on) for j in jobs], snapshot)
        self.assertIs(jobs[0].depends_on, deps)

    def test_accepts_any_iterable(self):
        jobs = (Job(jid) for jid in ("a", "b"))
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b"])

    def test_repeated_dependency_edges_still_work(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("top", depends_on=("base", "base")),
            Job("base"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["base", "top"])

    def test_long_chain(self):
        count = 200
        jobs = [Job("j0")] + [
            Job(f"j{i}", depends_on=(f"j{i - 1}",)) for i in range(1, count)
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), [f"j{i}" for i in range(count)])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

deepseek-v4-pro/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs implements a deterministic topological sort via Kahn's algorithm, driven by a min-heap to respect the priority/input-order contract:

  1. Input snapshot: the iterable is copied to a list so the supplied jobs and their depends_on lists are never mutated.
  2. Validation:
    • every Job.id must be a non-empty str and unique, else ScheduleError;
    • every dependency must name a supplied job, else ScheduleError with the offending job and dependency in the message.
  3. Graph construction: builds dependents adjacency and indegree counts.
  4. Scheduling: a heap of ready jobs keyed by (-priority, input_index, id) yields higher priority first, with ties broken by original input order. When a job completes, its dependents' indegree drops; newly ready jobs are pushed.
  5. Cycle detection: if fewer jobs are emitted than supplied, the remaining positive-indegree IDs are collected and reported in the ScheduleError.

Edge cases covered

  • Dependencies override priority.
  • Priority then input order among ready jobs (including negative priorities).
  • Newly-ready jobs ordered by priority.
  • Multiple dependencies and transitive chains.
  • Direct two-node, longer, and self-dependency cycles (IDs included in message).
  • Unknown dependency rejection.
  • Duplicate, empty, and non-string job IDs rejected.
  • Empty input returns [].
  • Input (jobs and dependency lists) is not mutated.

Verification

Command:

python -m unittest -v test_jobqueue.py

Result:

Ran 15 tests in 0.003s

OK

deepseek-v4-pro/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

deepseek-v4-pro/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable
import heapq


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order."""
    job_list = list(jobs)

    seen = set()
    for job in job_list:
        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or job.id == "":
            raise ScheduleError(f"job id must be a non-empty string, got {job.id!r}")
        if job.id in seen:
            raise ScheduleError(f"duplicate job id: {job.id!r}")
        seen.add(job.id)

    id_to_index = {job.id: i for i, job in enumerate(job_list)}

    for job in job_list:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            if dep not in id_to_index:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}"
                )

    dependents = {job.id: [] for job in job_list}
    indegree = {job.id: 0 for job in job_list}
    for job in job_list:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            dependents[dep].append(job.id)
            indegree[job.id] += 1

    heap = []
    for i, job in enumerate(job_list):
        if indegree[job.id] == 0:
            heapq.heappush(heap, (-job.priority, i, job.id))

    result = []
    while heap:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(heap)
        result.append(job_id)
        for nxt in dependents[job_id]:
            indegree[nxt] -= 1
            if indegree[nxt] == 0:
                i = id_to_index[nxt]
                heapq.heappush(heap, (-job_list[i].priority, i, nxt))

    if len(result) != len(job_list):
        cycle_ids = sorted(job_id for job_id, d in indegree.items() if d > 0)
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"dependency cycle detected involving: {', '.join(cycle_ids)}"
        )

    return result

deepseek-v4-pro/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_transitive_dependency_chain(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("c"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["c", "b", "a"])

    def test_priority_orders_newly_ready_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", priority=1, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("b", priority=9, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("c", priority=5, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("root", priority=0),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "b", "c", "a"])

    def test_multiple_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("deploy", depends_on=("test", "build")),
            Job("test", depends_on=("build",)),
            Job("build"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["build", "test", "deploy"])

    def test_direct_cycle_is_rejected_with_ids(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))
        self.assertIn("b", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_self_dependency_cycle_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])

    def test_longer_cycle_is_rejected(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("b",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs(jobs)

    def test_duplicate_job_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("a")])

    def test_empty_job_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_non_string_job_id_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(123)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_input_is_not_mutated(self):
        dep_list = ["b"]
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=tuple(dep_list)), Job("b")]
        snapshot = [(job.id, job.priority, tuple(job.depends_on)) for job in jobs]
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(
            [(job.id, job.priority, tuple(job.depends_on)) for job in jobs],
            snapshot,
        )

    def test_negative_priorities_are_honored(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", priority=-1), Job("b", priority=-5), Job("c", priority=0)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["c", "a", "b"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

mimo/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

RESPONSE — Task 01: dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs is a deterministic Kahn topological sort with a priority queue.

  1. Materialize and validate. The iterable is copied to a list (generators work). Each Job.id must be a non-empty str and unique; violations raise ScheduleError. Dependencies are validated against the supplied ID set. Duplicate dependency entries are ignored after the first so they cannot skew in-degree counts.
  2. Graph build. For each job, a local seen set dedupes depends_on while filling dependents and per-job remaining-dependency counts. Dependencies are read via tuple(job.depends_on), so the caller’s sequence is never mutated (and Job is a frozen dataclass).
  3. Ready selection. A binary heap of (-priority, input_index, id) pops the best currently ready job: higher priority first, equal priority by original input order. Newly unlocked jobs re-enter the same heap, so priority is re-evaluated among whatever is ready at that moment. Dependencies always win over priority: a job is never scheduled before its deps, even at priority=100.
  4. Cycle detection. If the sort finishes early, the remaining IDs are exactly the unresolvable set (cycle members plus jobs that depend on them). A small DFS over that subgraph extracts one concrete cycle path for the diagnostic, e.g. dependency cycle detected: a -> b -> c -> a (unresolvable jobs: a, b, c). Self-dependencies surface as a -> a. No cycle helper mutates inputs.

Empty input returns []. Output length equals input length on success (each ID once). Standard library only (heapq, dataclasses, typing).

Edge cases covered in test_jobqueue.py

Area Cases
Core contract empty input; single job; every ID exactly once; generator input
Ordering deps override priority; ready set by priority then input order; priority applied when a job becomes ready; equal priority keeps input order for late-ready jobs; negative priority
Structure diamond dependency; duplicate depends_on entries
Validation unknown dependency (message names both jobs); duplicate job ID; empty job ID; non-string job ID
Cycles self-dependency; 2-cycle; 3-cycle with a bystander job in the plan; cycle with a dependent stuck outside the cycle — IDs appear in the diagnostic
Immutability jobs and their dependency tuples unchanged after planning; caller-owned list unchanged

The three original tests are preserved unchanged.

Verification

Command (from this directory):

python3 -m unittest test_jobqueue -v

Result:

Ran 21 tests in 0.001s

OK

All 21 tests passed. No further checks were required by the task (stdlib only; no linter or type-checker config was supplied).

mimo/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

mimo/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from __future__ import annotations

import heapq
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order.

    Among ready jobs, higher ``priority`` runs first; ties keep input order.
    """
    job_list = list(jobs)
    if not job_list:
        return []

    by_id: dict[str, Job] = {}
    input_index: dict[str, int] = {}
    for index, job in enumerate(job_list):
        job_id = job.id
        if not isinstance(job_id, str) or not job_id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"job id must be a non-empty string, got {job_id!r}")
        if job_id in by_id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"duplicate job id: {job_id!r}")
        by_id[job_id] = job
        input_index[job_id] = index

    # dependents[d] = jobs that list d as a dependency; remaining counts unmet deps.
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {job_id: [] for job_id in by_id}
    remaining: dict[str, int] = {job_id: 0 for job_id in by_id}

    for job in job_list:
        # Copy so a caller-supplied mutable sequence is never touched.
        seen: set[str] = set()
        for dep in tuple(job.depends_on):
            if dep not in by_id:
                raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}")
            if dep in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(dep)
            dependents[dep].append(job.id)
            remaining[job.id] += 1

    # Kahn's algorithm; ready set ordered by (-priority, input_index, id).
    ready: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []
    for job_id, unmet in remaining.items():
        if unmet == 0:
            job = by_id[job_id]
            heapq.heappush(ready, (-job.priority, input_index[job_id], job_id))

    order: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(ready)
        order.append(job_id)
        for dependent in dependents[job_id]:
            remaining[dependent] -= 1
            if remaining[dependent] == 0:
                job = by_id[dependent]
                heapq.heappush(
                    ready, (-job.priority, input_index[dependent], dependent)
                )

    if len(order) != len(by_id):
        stuck = sorted(job_id for job_id, unmet in remaining.items() if unmet > 0)
        cycle = _find_cycle(stuck, by_id)
        detail = " -> ".join(cycle) if cycle else ", ".join(stuck)
        raise ScheduleError(
            f"dependency cycle detected: {detail}"
            f" (unresolvable jobs: {', '.join(stuck)})"
        )

    return order


def _find_cycle(stuck: list[str], by_id: dict[str, Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return one dependency cycle as [id, ..., id], or [] if none found."""
    stuck_set = set(stuck)
    color = dict.fromkeys(stuck, 0)  # 0=unvisited, 1=on stack, 2=done

    def dfs(node: str, path: list[str]) -> list[str]:
        color[node] = 1
        path.append(node)
        for dep in dict.fromkeys(by_id[node].depends_on):
            if dep not in stuck_set:
                continue
            if color[dep] == 1:
                start = path.index(dep)
                return path[start:]
            if color[dep] == 0:
                found = dfs(dep, path)
                if found:
                    return found
        path.pop()
        color[node] = 2
        return []

    for job_id in stuck:
        if color[job_id] == 0:
            found = dfs(job_id, [])
            if found:
                return found
    return []

mimo/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])

    def test_all_ids_returned_exactly_once(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("b", depends_on=("d",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("d",)),
            Job("d"),
        ]
        order = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(sorted(order), ["a", "b", "c", "d"])
        self.assertEqual(len(order), 4)

    def test_diamond_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("top", depends_on=("left", "right")),
            Job("left", depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("right", depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("root"),
        ]
        order = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(order[0], "root")
        self.assertEqual(order[-1], "top")
        self.assertLess(order.index("left"), order.index("top"))
        self.assertLess(order.index("right"), order.index("top"))

    def test_priority_applies_when_job_becomes_ready(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("x", priority=1, depends_on=("z",)),
            Job("y", priority=2),
            Job("z", priority=0),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["y", "z", "x"])

    def test_equal_priority_keeps_input_order_for_late_ready_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("late1", priority=3, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("late2", priority=3, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("root", priority=0),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "late1", "late2"])

    def test_negative_priority_orders_last_among_ready(self):
        jobs = [Job("low", priority=-5), Job("high", priority=0)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["high", "low"])

    def test_duplicate_job_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("a")])
        self.assertIn("duplicate", str(ctx.exception).lower())

    def test_empty_job_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_non_string_job_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(123)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_unknown_dependency_message_names_jobs(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("build", message)
        self.assertIn("missing", message)

    def test_self_dependency_is_a_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_two_job_cycle(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("cycle", message.lower())
        self.assertIn("a", message)
        self.assertIn("b", message)

    def test_longer_cycle_reports_involved_ids(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("ok"),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        for job_id in ("a", "b", "c"):
            self.assertIn(job_id, message)

    def test_cycle_with_bystander_dependency(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        message = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", message)
        self.assertIn("b", message)

    def test_duplicate_entries_in_depends_on(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", depends_on=("fetch", "fetch")),
            Job("fetch"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_does_not_mutate_jobs_or_dependencies(self):
        deps = ["fetch"]
        jobs = [Job("build", depends_on=tuple(deps)), Job("fetch", priority=1)]
        snapshot = [(job.id, job.priority, job.depends_on) for job in jobs]
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(
            [(job.id, job.priority, job.depends_on) for job in jobs], snapshot
        )
        self.assertEqual(deps, ["fetch"])

    def test_generator_input(self):
        def gen():
            yield Job("b", priority=1, depends_on=("a",))
            yield Job("a", priority=9)

        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(gen()), ["a", "b"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

Evaluation 7.75 / 10 graded blind as submission W

Correct output with good input validation, but each step picks the next job with max() and list.remove() over the ready list, which is O(n^2). 10,000 independent jobs take 19.5s and 50,000 time out. The cycle message lists all blocked jobs. Tests never make a newly-ready job compete, so FIFO and tie mutants survive.

Strengths

  • Validates bare-string, None and non-string dependencies and non-Job items
  • Correct on all ordering probes at small sizes

Weaknesses

  • O(n^2) scheduling: 5,000 jobs 3.9s, 10,000 jobs 19.5s, 50,000 > 20s timeout
  • Cycle message includes downstream jobs
  • Tests survive fifo, levels, tie_by_id and tie_ready_time

muse/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Response — dependency-aware scheduler

Design

plan_jobs validates then runs Kahn's topological sort. It copies the input to a list (supports any iterable, preserves input order), validates every ID is a unique non-empty string, validates every dependency names a supplied job, builds indegree/dependents maps with duplicate dependencies deduplicated, then repeatedly picks from currently-ready jobs via max(ready, key=(priority, -input_index)) so higher priority wins and ties keep input order. Newly unblocked jobs join ready; if output is short, the remaining IDs (input order) are reported in a ScheduleError cycle message. Only reads inputs (list() copies); never mutates jobs or dependency lists. Empty input returns []. Standard library only.

Edge cases covered

Added 20 tests to test_jobqueue.py (23 total): empty tuple/list, single job, duplicate IDs, empty/non-string IDs, unknown dependency + message contents, invalid deps ("", non-string, None, bare-string depends_on), self-cycle, 2-cycle and 4-node cycle ID reporting, duplicate deps tolerated, diamond, chain overriding priority, newly-ready high priority jumping ahead, equal priority after unblock, no-mutation of jobs and of list-valued depends_on, generator input, and every-ID-once plus topological validity on a 5-node graph.

Verification

Command (run in this directory):

python3 -m unittest test_jobqueue -v

Result: Ran 23 tests in 0.001s — OK.

muse/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

muse/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order."""
    if jobs is None:
        raise ScheduleError("jobs must be an iterable of Job, got None")
    try:
        job_list = list(jobs)
    except TypeError as exc:
        raise ScheduleError(f"jobs must be an iterable of Job: {exc}") from exc
    if not job_list:
        return []

    id_to_job: dict[str, Job] = {}
    id_to_index: dict[str, int] = {}
    id_to_priority: dict[str, int] = {}
    for index, job in enumerate(job_list):
        if not isinstance(job, Job):
            raise ScheduleError(f"item at index {index} is not a Job: {job!r}")
        jid = job.id
        if not isinstance(jid, str) or not jid:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job at index {index} has invalid id {jid!r}: must be a non-empty string"
            )
        if jid in id_to_job:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"duplicate job id {jid!r} at index {index} "
                f"(first seen at index {id_to_index[jid]})"
            )
        id_to_job[jid] = job
        id_to_index[jid] = index
        id_to_priority[jid] = job.priority

    indegree: dict[str, int] = {jid: 0 for jid in id_to_job}
    dependents: dict[str, list[str]] = {jid: [] for jid in id_to_job}
    for job in job_list:
        raw = job.depends_on
        if raw is None:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job {job.id!r} has invalid depends_on None: must be an iterable of job IDs"
            )
        if isinstance(raw, (str, bytes)):
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job {job.id!r} has invalid depends_on {raw!r}: "
                "must be an iterable of job IDs, not a string"
            )
        try:
            dep_list = list(raw)
        except TypeError as exc:
            raise ScheduleError(
                f"job {job.id!r} has invalid depends_on {raw!r}: must be an iterable of job IDs"
            ) from exc
        seen: set[str] = set()
        for dep in dep_list:
            if not isinstance(dep, str) or not dep:
                raise ScheduleError(
                    f"job {job.id!r} has invalid dependency {dep!r}: must be a non-empty string"
                )
            if dep not in id_to_job:
                raise ScheduleError(f"job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}")
            if dep in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(dep)
            indegree[job.id] += 1
            dependents[dep].append(job.id)

    ready = [jid for jid, deg in indegree.items() if deg == 0]
    result: list[str] = []
    while ready:
        best = max(ready, key=lambda jid: (id_to_priority[jid], -id_to_index[jid]))
        ready.remove(best)
        result.append(best)
        for child in dependents[best]:
            indegree[child] -= 1
            if indegree[child] == 0:
                ready.append(child)

    if len(result) != len(job_list):
        remaining = sorted(
            (jid for jid, deg in indegree.items() if deg > 0),
            key=lambda jid: id_to_index[jid],
        )
        detail = ", ".join(repr(j) for j in remaining)
        raise ScheduleError(f"dependency cycle detected involving: {detail}")

    return result

muse/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(()), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("solo", priority=3)]), ["solo"])

    def test_duplicate_ids_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a"), Job("b"), Job("a")])
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_empty_string_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_non_string_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(123)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_unknown_dependency_message_is_useful(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])
        msg = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("build", msg)
        self.assertIn("missing", msg)

    def test_invalid_dependency_entries_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("",))])
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=(123,))])  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_string_depends_on_rejected(self):
        # A bare string must not be treated as an iterable of IDs.
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on="b"), Job("b")])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_self_dependency_is_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])
        self.assertIn("a", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_direct_cycle_reports_ids(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b", depends_on=("a",))]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        msg = str(ctx.exception)
        self.assertIn("a", msg)
        self.assertIn("b", msg)

    def test_longer_cycle_reports_ids(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("ok"),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as ctx:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        msg = str(ctx.exception)
        for jid in ("a", "b", "c"):
            self.assertIn(jid, msg)

    def test_duplicate_dependencies_tolerated(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b", "b")), Job("b")]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a"])

    def test_diamond_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a"),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("d", depends_on=("b", "c")),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c", "d"])

    def test_chain_overrides_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("c", priority=100, depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", priority=50, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_newly_ready_high_priority_jumps_ahead(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("low", priority=1),
            Job("blocked_high", priority=100, depends_on=("low",)),
            Job("mid", priority=50),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["mid", "low", "blocked_high"])

    def test_equal_priority_keeps_input_order_after_unblock(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("root"),
            Job("x", priority=5, depends_on=("root",)),
            Job("y", priority=5, depends_on=("root",)),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["root", "x", "y"])

    def test_does_not_mutate_inputs(self):
        jobs = [Job("b", priority=2), Job("a", priority=1, depends_on=("b",))]
        snapshot = [(j.id, j.priority, tuple(j.depends_on)) for j in jobs]
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual([(j.id, j.priority, tuple(j.depends_on)) for j in jobs], snapshot)

    def test_does_not_mutate_list_depends_on(self):
        deps = ["y"]
        jobs = [Job("x", depends_on=deps), Job("y")]  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["y", "x"])
        self.assertEqual(deps, ["y"])

    def test_accepts_generator_input(self):
        def gen():
            yield Job("b", priority=1)
            yield Job("a", priority=9, depends_on=("b",))

        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(gen()), ["b", "a"])

    def test_returns_every_id_exactly_once(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", priority=2),
            Job("b", priority=3, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", priority=3),
            Job("d", priority=1, depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("e", priority=9, depends_on=("d",)),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(sorted(result), ["a", "b", "c", "d", "e"])
        pos = {jid: i for i, jid in enumerate(result)}
        for job in jobs:
            for dep in job.depends_on:
                self.assertLess(pos[dep], pos[job.id])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

nemotron-3-ultra/01-feature-building/RESPONSE.md

Response: Dependency-Aware Scheduler Implementation

Design Summary

The plan_jobs function implements a topological sort using Kahn's algorithm with a priority queue (min-heap) to satisfy the ordering constraints:

  1. Graph Construction: Jobs are converted to a directed graph where edges represent dependencies. In-degree counts track how many dependencies each job has remaining.

  2. Priority Queue for Ready Jobs: Jobs with zero in-degree (no remaining dependencies) are pushed into a min-heap keyed by (-priority, input_index, job_id). The negative priority ensures higher priorities pop first; the input index breaks ties deterministically by original input order.

  3. Processing Loop: Repeatedly pop the highest-priority ready job, append to result, decrement in-degree of its dependents, and push newly-ready jobs onto the heap.

  4. Cycle Detection: After the loop, if the result length doesn't match the input count, a cycle exists. The error message lists all jobs not in the result (those involved in the cycle).

  5. Input Validation: Validates non-empty string IDs, uniqueness, and that all dependencies reference known jobs—all before any scheduling.

  6. Immutability: The input jobs and their depends_on tuples are never mutated; all state is tracked in separate dictionaries.

Edge Cases Covered

Test Description
test_empty_input_returns_empty_list Empty iterable returns []
test_single_job Single job with no dependencies
test_chain_of_dependencies Linear dependency chain
test_multiple_dependencies Job depends on multiple predecessors
test_diamond_dependencies Diamond DAG pattern (a→b, a→c, b→d, c→d)
test_priority_ordering_among_ready Higher priority chosen first when multiple ready
test_equal_priority_uses_input_order Stable ordering by input index for equal priority
test_priority_with_dependencies Priority only applies among ready jobs
test_cycle_detection_two_jobs 2-node cycle (a→b→a)
test_cycle_detection_three_jobs 3-node cycle (a→b→c→a)
test_self_dependency_cycle Job depending on itself
test_duplicate_job_id_rejected Duplicate IDs raise ScheduleError
test_empty_job_id_rejected Empty string ID rejected
test_none_job_id_rejected Non-string ID rejected
test_input_not_mutated Input list unchanged after call
test_dependency_lists_not_mutated depends_on tuples unchanged

Verification

Command:

python test_jobqueue.py

Result:

OK

All 20 tests pass, covering the core contract requirements and additional edge cases.

nemotron-3-ultra/01-feature-building/TASK.md

Task 01 — Feature building: dependency-aware scheduler

Implement plan_jobs in jobqueue.py and expand test_jobqueue.py with useful edge cases. Use only the Python standard library.

Contract:

  • Every Job.id must be a non-empty string and unique.
  • Every dependency must name a supplied job. Reject invalid input with ScheduleError and a useful message.
  • Return every job ID exactly once, always after all of its dependencies.
  • Among jobs currently ready, choose higher priority first. Break equal priorities by original input order.
  • Detect dependency cycles and raise ScheduleError; include the IDs involved in the diagnostic when practical.
  • Do not mutate the supplied jobs or their dependency lists.
  • Empty input returns an empty list.

Run the tests. Write RESPONSE.md summarizing the design, edge cases covered, and the exact verification command/result. Keep all work inside this directory.

nemotron-3-ultra/01-feature-building/jobqueue.py

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Iterable
import heapq


class ScheduleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Job:
    id: str
    priority: int = 0
    depends_on: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    payload: Any = None


def plan_jobs(jobs: Iterable[Job]) -> list[str]:
    """Return job IDs in a valid deterministic execution order."""
    jobs_list = list(jobs)
    
    if not jobs_list:
        return []
    
    job_ids = set()
    for job in jobs_list:
        if not isinstance(job.id, str) or not job.id:
            raise ScheduleError(f"Invalid job ID: {job.id!r}")
        if job.id in job_ids:
            raise ScheduleError(f"Duplicate job ID: {job.id!r}")
        job_ids.add(job.id)
    
    for job in jobs_list:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            if dep not in job_ids:
                raise ScheduleError(f"Job {job.id!r} depends on unknown job {dep!r}")
    
    in_degree = {job.id: len(job.depends_on) for job in jobs_list}
    adjacency = {job.id: [] for job in jobs_list}
    
    for job in jobs_list:
        for dep in job.depends_on:
            adjacency[dep].append(job.id)
    
    job_index = {job.id: idx for idx, job in enumerate(jobs_list)}
    job_priority = {job.id: job.priority for job in jobs_list}
    
    ready = []
    for job_id, deg in in_degree.items():
        if deg == 0:
            heapq.heappush(ready, (-job_priority[job_id], job_index[job_id], job_id))
    
    result = []
    
    while ready:
        _, _, job_id = heapq.heappop(ready)
        result.append(job_id)
        
        for successor in adjacency[job_id]:
            in_degree[successor] -= 1
            if in_degree[successor] == 0:
                heapq.heappush(ready, (-job_priority[successor], job_index[successor], successor))
    
    if len(result) != len(jobs_list):
        remaining = [job_id for job_id in job_ids if job_id not in result]
        raise ScheduleError(f"Dependency cycle detected involving: {', '.join(remaining)}")
    
    return result

nemotron-3-ultra/01-feature-building/test_jobqueue.py

import unittest

from jobqueue import Job, ScheduleError, plan_jobs


class PlanJobsTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_dependencies_override_priority(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("build", priority=100, depends_on=("fetch",)),
            Job("fetch", priority=1),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["fetch", "build"])

    def test_ready_jobs_use_priority_then_input_order(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", 2), Job("b", 5), Job("c", 2)]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["b", "a", "c"])

    def test_unknown_dependency_is_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("build", depends_on=("missing",))])

    def test_empty_input_returns_empty_list(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([]), [])

    def test_single_job(self):
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs([Job("only")]), ["only"])

    def test_chain_of_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("c", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c"])

    def test_multiple_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("d", depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a"),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["a", "b", "c", "d"])

    def test_diamond_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("d", depends_on=("b", "c")),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a"),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(result[0], "a")
        self.assertEqual(result[-1], "d")
        self.assertIn("b", result)
        self.assertIn("c", result)
        self.assertLess(result.index("b"), result.index("d"))
        self.assertLess(result.index("c"), result.index("d"))

    def test_priority_ordering_among_ready(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("low", priority=1),
            Job("high", priority=10),
            Job("mid", priority=5),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["high", "mid", "low"])

    def test_equal_priority_uses_input_order(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("first", priority=5),
            Job("second", priority=5),
            Job("third", priority=5),
        ]
        self.assertEqual(plan_jobs(jobs), ["first", "second", "third"])

    def test_priority_with_dependencies(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", priority=1),
            Job("b", priority=10, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", priority=5, depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(result[0], "a")
        self.assertEqual(result[1], "b")
        self.assertEqual(result[2], "c")

    def test_cycle_detection_two_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("a", str(cm.exception))
        self.assertIn("b", str(cm.exception))

    def test_cycle_detection_three_jobs(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("a", depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("b", depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("c", depends_on=("b",)),
        ]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("a", str(cm.exception))
        self.assertIn("b", str(cm.exception))
        self.assertIn("c", str(cm.exception))

    def test_self_dependency_cycle(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("a", depends_on=("a",))])

    def test_duplicate_job_id_rejected(self):
        jobs = [Job("same"), Job("same")]
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError) as cm:
            plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertIn("Duplicate", str(cm.exception))

    def test_empty_job_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job("")])

    def test_none_job_id_rejected(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ScheduleError):
            plan_jobs([Job(None)])  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    def test_input_not_mutated(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b")]
        original_depends = jobs[0].depends_on
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(jobs[0].depends_on, original_depends)

    def test_dependency_lists_not_mutated(self):
        jobs = [Job("a", depends_on=("b",)), Job("b")]
        original_deps = list(jobs[0].depends_on)
        plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(list(jobs[0].depends_on), original_deps)

    def test_complex_graph_with_priorities(self):
        jobs = [
            Job("f", priority=1, depends_on=("d", "e")),
            Job("e", priority=5, depends_on=("c",)),
            Job("d", priority=10, depends_on=("b",)),
            Job("c", priority=2, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("b", priority=8, depends_on=("a",)),
            Job("a", priority=3),
        ]
        result = plan_jobs(jobs)
        self.assertEqual(result[0], "a")
        self.assertEqual(result[-1], "f")
        self.assertLess(result.index("b"), result.index("d"))
        self.assertLess(result.index("c"), result.index("e"))
        self.assertLess(result.index("d"), result.index("f"))
        self.assertLess(result.index("e"), result.index("f"))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

Evaluation 7 / 10 graded blind as submission A

Heap-based Kahn scheduler that is correct on ordinary input but breaks when a depends_on contains duplicate entries: pending is a set while dependents are appended once per entry, so the dependent is pushed several times. Valid input is then rejected with an empty cycle list, or duplicate IDs are returned and a real cycle is missed. The suite is mid-sized and does not catch this or the core ready-time priority mutants.

Strengths

  • Validation of empty, non-string and duplicate IDs with position in the message
  • Hidden contract tests 5/5; 20 own tests pass
  • Cycle message lists sorted blocked ids

Weaknesses

  • Duplicate dependency entries: plan_jobs([Job('b',0,('a','a')),Job('a')]) raises 'Dependency cycle detected involving jobs: []'
  • With b depends_on ('a','a','a') plus an x<->y cycle it silently returns ['a','b','b','b']: duplicate IDs and a missed cycle
  • Unhashable dependency entry raises TypeError rather than ScheduleError
  • Tests survive the fifo, levels, tie_ready_time, unknown_no_names, list-mutation and dup_dep_bug mutants
Evidence the grader checked
  • jobqueue.py:83 pending = set(job.depends_on) vs :87 dependents[dep].append per occurrence
  • probe dup_dep_entries -> 'EXC ScheduleError: Dependency cycle detected involving jobs: []'
  • manual run: [Job('a'),Job('b',0,('a','a','a')),Job('x',0,('y',)),Job('y',0,('x',))] -> ['a','b','b','b']
  • cross-test: H's test_duplicate_entries_in_depends_on fails on A
  • mutation run (reference impl + 18 mutants, mut/run.py + run2.py): 12/18 killed
